Existing UNet-based models often struggle to produce clear and coherent text, while DiT-based models, though more expressive, are typically limited to a single task, which may lead to redundant training pipelines, inconsistent visual styles, and reduced cross-task generalization. To address these challenges, we propose InnoText, a unified DiT-based framework capable of performing both text generation and editing within a single model. We introduce a Font Size-Aware Modulation (FSAM) module to enhance representations across font scales, a Small-Character Aware Augmentation strategy to improve fine-grained fidelity, and a Task-Specific Region Weighted Loss for adaptive optimization. To support training and evaluation, we also construct a high-quality bilingual (English-Chinese) visual text dataset covering diverse fonts, sizes, and backgrounds. Experimental results demonstrate that our method achieves superior generation accuracy and editing quality, producing visually appealing and realistic text images.

</details>


### [27] [Projection Pursuit CPCANet for Domain Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22117)
*Yu-Hsi Chen,Abd-Krim Seghouane*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PP-CPCANet addresses the rank-deficient covariance estimation problem in CPCANet by introducing a covariance-free framework that learns a global orthogonal basis on the Stiefel manifold via Cayley transform, using a symmetry-breaking detached-median PP dispersion objective, achieving SOTA on four DG benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: CPCANet suffers from rank-deficient covariance estimation due to the small-sample-size issue in mini-batch training, limiting its ability to extract domain-invariant structures.

Method: Proposes a covariance-free framework that learns a global orthogonal basis on the Stiefel manifold via Cayley transform, jointly optimized with network parameters. Introduces a symmetry-breaking detached-median Projection Pursuit dispersion objective for extracting CPCs.

Result: Achieves SOTA performance on four Domain Generalization benchmarks with stable training.

Conclusion: The proposed covariance-free approach effectively overcomes the small-sample-size limitation of CPCANet, yielding superior domain generalization performance.

Abstract: Domain Generalization (DG) aims to learn representations robust to distribution shifts. Recent geometric alignment methods, such as CPCANet, extract domain-invariant structures through batch-wise Common Principal Component Analysis (CPCA). However, CPCANet suffers from rank-deficient covariance estimation due to the small-sample-size issue in mini-batch training. To address this limitation, we propose Projection Pursuit CPCANet (PP-CPCANet), a covariance-free framework that learns a global orthogonal basis on the Stiefel manifold and jointly optimizes it with network parameters via the Cayley transform. We further introduce a symmetry-breaking detached-median PP dispersion objective to extract common principal components (CPCs) with dense and robust optimization signals. Experiments on four DG benchmarks show that PP-CPCANet achieves SOTA performance while maintaining stable training.

</details>


### [28] [A Framework for Individual Tree Growth Reconstruction Using Multi-Platform Laser Scanning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22129)
*Daniella Tavi,Valtteri Soininen,Lassi Ruoppa,Jesse Muhojoki,Juha Hyyppä*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This study develops a framework for estimating individual tree DBH and stem volume growth using 136 multitemporal point clouds (2014-2025) from ALS, MLS, and TLS across boreal forests. Deep learning-based segmentation from MLS ensures reliable tree correspondence. A height growth-based scaling model reconstructs stem attributes across time, achieving higher agreement with manual growth estimates than direct differencing of point cloud attributes. Modeled-manual RMSEs for 5- and 10-year growth were 55-111% and 26-67% for DBH, and 31-87% and 21-67% for volume. The model stabilizes after 5-6 years, with maximum 12-year RMSEs of 8-12% for DBH and 12-23% for volume.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate forest monitoring at tree level requires reliable tree delineation, consistent multitemporal correspondence, and precise estimation of tree attributes and their change. Reconstructing tree growth in boreal forests is challenging due to scarcity of historical stem-level data, error propagation from older sensors, and growth rates near measurement uncertainty. This study aims to address these challenges by developing a robust framework for individual tree growth estimation using diverse laser scanning data.

Method: 136 point clouds (2014-2025) from 11 scanners on ALS, MLS, and TLS platforms across boreal forest test sites were used. Trees were delineated from MLS using deep learning-based segmentation, then transferred to other point clouds for consistent correspondence. Stem curves from MLS/TLS and heights from ALS enabled DBH and volume estimation. A height growth-based scaling model reconstructed stem attributes across time and estimated growth.

Result: Modeled growth outperformed direct differencing of independently estimated attributes. Modeled-manual 5- and 10-year growth RMSEs: 55-111% and 26-67% for DBH; 31-87% and 21-67% for volume (plot-dependent). The scaling model stabilized after 5-6 years, with maximum 12-year RMSEs of 8-12% for DBH and 12-23% for volume.

Conclusion: Combining MLS/TLS-derived stem measurements with multitemporal ALS-derived heights provides a robust framework for individual tree growth estimation without requiring multiple under-canopy scans. The height growth-based scaling model is temporally robust and yields more reliable growth estimates than direct attribute differencing.

Abstract: Accurate tree-level forest monitoring using laser scanning data requires reliable tree delineation, consistent tree correspondence across multitemporal point clouds, and accurate estimation of tree attributes and their change. Reconstructing tree growth in boreal forests is challenging due to the scarcity of historical stem-level data, propagation of errors from older sensors into change estimation, and growth rates with a magnitude of measurement uncertainty. This study investigates a framework for estimating individual tree diameter at breast height (DBH) and stem volume growth using 136 point clouds acquired between 2014--2025 with 11 scanners on airborne (ALS), mobile (MLS), and terrestrial laser scanning (TLS) platforms across boreal forest test sites. Trees were delineated from an MLS point cloud using deep learning-based segmentation which was transferred to the remaining point clouds, resulting in reliable multitemporal tree correspondence. Stem curves were derived from MLS/TLS data, with ALS data used for height estimation, enabling DBH and volume estimation and time series. A height growth-based scaling model was used to reconstruct stem attributes across time and estimate growth. Results showed that modeled growth achieved higher agreement with manual growth estimates than differencing independently estimated attributes from point clouds. The modeled-manual 5- and 10-year growth RMSEs were 55--111\% and 26--67\% for DBH, and 31--87\% and 21--67\% for volume, respectively, depending on plot difficulty. The scaling model was temporally robust, with errors remaining stable or stabilizing after 5--6 years, reaching maximum RMSEs of 8--12\% for DBH and 12--23\% for volume after 12 years. Combining MLS/TLS-derived stem measurements with multitemporal ALS-derived heights provided a robust framework for individual tree growth estimation without requiring multiple under-canopy scans.

</details>


### [29] [GLI-AL: A Multi-Modal Glioma MRI Label Resource with Unified Anatomy-Lesion Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22135)
*Xingyu Xiang,Shuang Hao,Fan Wang,Jianhua Ma,Chunfeng Lian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces BraTS-GLI Anatomy-Lesion, a label-only dataset derived from BraTS 2023-GLI, adding 8-class anatomy-lesion annotations (including healthy brain tissues and WMH) to address label noise from unlabeled coexisting abnormalities in glioma segmentation. It includes 1,251 cases with metadata, and validation shows WMH-aware supervision maintains healthy-tissue performance while improving coexisting lesion sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing BraTS-GLI datasets lack systematic representation of white matter hyperintensities (WMH), causing task-specific label noise in joint segmentation settings where unlabeled abnormalities are treated as normal tissue.

Method: Derived 1,251 unified eight-class anatomy-lesion label sets from BraTS 2023-GLI training cohort, organized into purified (394-case) and extended (857-case) subsets with metadata. Validated using MedNeXt and T1/FLAIR inputs for WMH-aware supervision.

Result: WMH-aware supervision preserves healthy-tissue segmentation performance on both in-domain GLI and external WMH datasets, and improves sensitivity to coexisting lesions compared to noisy-control training.

Conclusion: The resource supports joint anatomy-lesion supervision, label-noise analysis, and reproducible evaluation for scientific research.

Abstract: Existing BraTS-GLI datasets provide a widely used benchmark for adult glioma MRI segmentation, but their task definition focuses on tumor subregions and does not systematically represent coexisting white matter hyperintensities (WMH). In joint segmentation settings, such unlabeled abnormalities introduce task-specific label noise by treating pathological regions as normal tissue. To address this limitation, we introduce BraTS-GLI Anatomy-Lesion, a controlled-access, labels-only derived resource built from the BraTS 2023-GLI training cohort. The resource provides 1,251 unified eight-class anatomy-lesion label sets aligned with the original four-modal MRI cases, including image-repair labels for 116 cases requiring repaired imaging inputs. The cohort is organized into a 394-case purified subset and an 857-case extended subset, with case-level metadata covering label source, image-repair requirements, quality-control status, access conditions, checksums, and release boundaries. Compared with the original BraTS-GLI annotations, the resource substantially expands foreground supervision by incorporating healthy brain tissues and previously unlabeled coexisting abnormalities within a unified label space. A validation study using MedNeXt and T1/FLAIR inputs suggests that WMH-aware supervision preserves healthy-tissue segmentation performance across both in-domain GLI and external WMH datasets, while improving sensitivity to coexisting lesions relative to noisy-control training. The resource is intended for scientific research and supports joint anatomy-lesion supervision, label-noise analysis, and reproducible evaluation. Data are available at https://www.synapse.org/Synapse:syn75210889/wiki/, and code is available at https://github.com/xyx200/brats-gli-anatomy-lesion-code. The data resource DOI is https://doi.org/10.7303/SYN75210889.

</details>


### [30] [CARDIAG: A Dense Segment Classification Benchmark of Deep Learning Architectures for Coronary Angiography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22139)
*Dominik Bernard Lau,Hubert Malinowski,Jerzy Szyjut,Adam Brzeski,Tomasz Dziubich,Radosław Targoński,Tomasz Figatowski,Natalia Zielińska*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces CARDIAG, a multi-center, multi-label benchmark dataset for pixel-level classification of coronary angiograms into SYNTAX classes, evaluates 24 deep learning architectures, and identifies ConvNeXt V2 encoder with DeepLab V3 Plus decoder (ensembled with Mamba U-Net and FPN) as the top performer achieving macro F1=0.479.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The field lacks standardized evaluation protocols for accurate pixel-level classification of coronary angiograms, which is critical for cardiovascular disease assessment.

Method: Created CARDIAG dataset (multi-center, multi-label) with careful train/test splits, evaluated 24 architectures (from classic convnets to state-space vision models), computed metrics including diameter error, overlap, centerline quality, and calibration. Best architecture: ConvNeXt V2 encoder + DeepLab V3 Plus decoder, ensembled with Mamba U-Net and Feature Pyramid Network.

Result: Top single model achieved macro F1=0.456; ensemble increased to macro F1=0.479. All architectures were well-calibrated. High-resolution and low-resolution features both important for encoding.

Conclusion: The released benchmark enables robust and rigorous assessment of future proposals for SYNTAX segmentation, lesion detection, and related tasks.

Abstract: Accurate pixel-level classification of coronary angiograms is critical for cardiovascular disease assessment, yet the field lacks standardized evaluation protocols. In this work we demonstrate a new benchmark for the assessment of deep learning models which densely classify pixels of coronary angiograms to one of SYNTAX classes (or background). The evaluation covers 24 distinct architectures starting with classic convnets to recent state-space-based vision algorithms. We release CARDIAG - a multi-center, multi-label dataset which we carefully split to reliably compute metrics, accounting for diameter error, overlap, centerline quality and calibration. The data contains SYNTAX labels, binary, uncertainty and segmentation masks as well as intermediate frames together with the selected non-sensitive DICOM metadata. From the multitude of algorithms, we nominate ConvNeXt V2 encoder with DeepLab V3 Plus decoder as the best performing, achieving macro $F_1=0.456$, which we then ensemble with Mamba U-Net and Feature Pyramid Network, for an increased $F_1=0.479$. We demonstrate all the architectures to be well calibrated and determine the generalization of the top 5 methods, together with the data efficiency of these architectures. We highlight the importance of both high-resolution and low-resolution features in encoding. We also demonstrate the model correctness in the context of patient demographic, vessel sides and projection angle configurations. Overall the released benchmark allows for future studies to robustly and rigorously assess the proposals, not only for SYNTAX segmentation, but lesion detection and many more.

</details>


### [31] [Visual Relocalization from Sparse Views in Aliased and Low-Texture Environments via Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22147)
*Maria Peribañez,Javier Civera,Rudolph Triebel,Riccardo Giubilato*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A visual relocalization method for planetary-like terrains that directly estimates 6-DoF camera poses from a single image against a 3D Gaussian Splatting map, using a geometry-aware training strategy combining photometric loss with MVS and LiDAR depth supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Visual localization in planetary terrains is extremely challenging due to low texture, perceptual aliasing, harsh illumination, and sparse/weakly overlapping viewpoints from forward motion and unconstrained driving — conditions that cause standard image-to-image and image-to-map matching pipelines to fail.

Method: Proposes a relocalization method that replaces classical correspondence-based pipelines with direct camera pose estimation against a differentiable 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) map. The key contribution is a geometry-aware training strategy with joint photometric and geometric losses, where geometric supervision is provided by combining multi-view stereo (MVS) and LiDAR depths for the first time.

Result: The joint optimization yields a 3DGS model with better scene geometry fit, improving photometric and geometric consistency. Extensive experiments in planetary-analog environments show substantial gains in relocalization accuracy under challenging conditions.

Conclusion: The proposed method effectively addresses visual relocalization in difficult planetary terrains by leveraging differentiable 3DGS with multi-modal geometric supervision, outperforming classical matching-based pipelines.

Abstract: Visual localization becomes extremely challenging in planetary-like terrains characterized by low texture, perceptual aliasing, harsh illumination, and sparse, weakly overlapping viewpoints induced by forward rover motion and unconstrained driving directions. Under these conditions, state-of-the-art image-to-image and image-to-map matching pipelines suffer significant performance degradation. In this work, we propose a visual relocalization method that departs from classical correspondence-based pipelines by directly estimating camera poses against a differentiable map representation built with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS). Our key contribution is a geometry-aware training strategy that combines photometric and geometric losses, where the geometric supervision is provided for the first time by combining multi-view stereo (MVS) and LiDAR depths. We show that this joint optimization produces a 3DGS model that better fits the underlying scene geometry, leading to improved photometric and geometric consistency and more robust, accurate single-image 6-DoF pose estimation. Extensive experiments on data acquired in planetary-analog environments validate the effectiveness of our approach, showing substantial gains in relocalization accuracy under challenging conditions. Code is available at https://github.com/DLR-RM/multimodal-gsplat-relocalization.

</details>


### [32] [dRAE: Representation Autoencoder with Hyper-Spherical Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22148)
*Tianren Ma,Lin Long,Chuyan Chen,Mu Zhang,Junbo Zhao,Tong Zhang,Qixiang Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper identifies metric mismatch in Euclidean codebook objectives as the root cause of codebook collapse when quantizing high-dimensional visual representations. It proposes Hyper-Spherical Quantization (HSQ) using angular routing to decouple semantics from feature magnitude, achieving a discrete Representation Autoencoder (dRAE) with 100% codebook utilization, scalability to 131,072 tokens, and strong performance across understanding and generation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between high-dimensional visual representations and language models by discretization, addressing the challenge that existing quantization methods suffer from codebook collapse and fail to scale while preserving semantic coherence.

Method: Hyper-Spherical Quantization (HSQ) - decouples semantic content from feature magnitude via angular routing, preventing code assignment from being dominated by scale rather than meaning, producing a discrete Representation Autoencoder (dRAE).

Result: Consistent performance gains as vocabulary size scales to 131,072, 100% codebook utilization, simplified training pipeline, and strong performance across understanding and generation tasks.

Conclusion: The proposed HSQ resolves the metric mismatch problem in visual representation quantization by adopting angular routing, enabling scalable and semantically coherent discrete representations that bridge visual and language modalities.

Abstract: In this work, we aim to discretize the high-dimensional visual representations to bridge the gap with language models - a non-trivial challenge, as existing quantization methods suffer from codebook collapse, failing to scale while preserving semantic coherence. We identify the root cause as metric mismatch: standard Euclidean codebook objectives are fundamentally misaligned with the anisotropic geometry of representation space, leading to codebook embeddings with high-variance magnitude scales and uneven angular distributions that hinder scalability. To address this, we propose Hyper-Spherical Quantization (HSQ), which decouples semantic content from feature magnitude via angular routing, preventing code assignment from being dominated by scale rather than meaning. The resulting discrete Representation Autoencoder (dRAE) achieves high-fidelity reconstruction while preserving semantic integrity and supporting scalable codebook budget. Extensive experiments demonstrate consistent performance gains as the vocabulary size scales to 131{,}072, along with 100\% codebook utilization, simplified training pipeline, and strong performance across understanding and generation tasks.

</details>


### [33] [JustDepth: Real-Time Radar-Camera Depth Estimation with Single-Scan LiDAR Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22172)
*Wooyung Yun,Dongwook Kim,Soomok Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: JustDepth is a single-stage radar-camera depth estimator that uses fixed-width 1D radar representation, Height Fusion Block, lightweight GNN, and confidence decoder to achieve accuracy comparable to SOTA but with 39.7x faster inference and 66% fewer stripe artifacts on nuScenes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing radar-camera depth estimation pipelines are multi-stage or rely on auxiliary annotations, leading to high latency and limited portability, which is critical for autonomous systems requiring accurate yet low-latency depth.

Method: JustDepth proposes: (1) aggregating radar returns into a fixed-width 1D representation to decouple runtime from point count, (2) a Height Fusion Block for modality fusion, (3) a lightweight GNN for global depth propagation, (4) a training-only confidence decoder to stabilize learning, and (5) simple augmentations plus a Vertical-Horizontal Gradient Ratio (VHGR) metric to mitigate and quantify stripe artifacts.

Result: On nuScenes, JustDepth maintains accuracy compared to recent SOTA methods while reducing inference time by 39.7x and stripe artifacts by 66% as measured by VHGR.

Conclusion: JustDepth achieves efficient, low-latency, and accurate radar-camera depth estimation without auxiliary annotations, demonstrating strong practical viability for real-time autonomous systems.

Abstract: Accurate yet low-latency depth is essential for radar-camera perception in autonomous systems. Cameras provide rich appearance but lack metric scale, whereas automotive radar offers metric range but is sparse and noisy. Many pipelines are multi-stage or depend on auxiliary annotations, increasing latency and limiting portability. We introduce JustDepth, a single-stage radar-camera depth estimator trained only with radar, camera, and single-scan LiDAR. All radar returns are aggregated into a fixed-width 1D representation, decoupling runtime from point count. A Height Fusion Block fuses modalities, a lightweight GNN propagates depth globally, and a training-only confidence decoder stabilizes learning with zero test-time cost. We mitigate stripe artifacts via simple augmentations and quantify them using the Vertical-Horizontal Gradient Ratio (VHGR). On nuScenes, compared to recent state-of-the-art methods, JustDepth maintains accuracy while reducing inference time by 39.7x and stripe artifacts by 66% as measured by VHGR.

</details>


### [34] [Bowel Obstruction Detection and Localization on Abdominal CT with Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22173)
*Moritz Vandenhirtz,Andrea Agostini,Dana Belde,Mélanie Roschewitz,Ismaiel Chikh Bakri,Tilo Niemann,André Euler,Julia E Vogt*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A deep learning framework with multi-task learning jointly detects bowel obstruction and localizes its transition zone on CT scans, achieving 93% detection accuracy and 95% Hit@10 localization using an interpretable probabilistic selection mask.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rising diagnostic workloads necessitate automated detection of bowel obstruction on CT scans to accelerate diagnosis and improve patient outcomes, while existing methods lack reliable transition zone localization—a critical clinical landmark.

Method: A multi-task deep learning framework that jointly classifies bowel obstruction and localizes the transition zone, extended with an inherently interpretable classification method via learning a probabilistic selection mask that bases predictions solely on a small image region.

Result: On an internal dataset of 1,427 abdominal CTs, the model achieved 93% obstruction detection test accuracy and 95% Hit@10 transition zone localization accuracy.

Conclusion: First method to reliably localize the transition zone for bowel obstruction, marking a significant step toward automated identification of this critical clinical landmark.

Abstract: Bowel obstruction is a common and potentially life-threatening gastrointestinal condition. In the face of rising diagnostic workloads, the automated diagnosis of bowel obstruction on CT scans supports radiologists by accelerating detection and improving patient outcomes. In this work, we propose a deep learning framework with a multi-task objective that jointly detects bowel obstruction and localizes its transition zone. Additionally, we extend the method with an inherently interpretable classification method that locates the suspected transition point within a slice. It does so by learning a probabilistic selection mask that faithfully bases the classifier's prediction solely on a small image region. The proposed method is evaluated on an internal dataset comprising 1,427 abdominal CTs. Here, the model achieves an obstruction detection test accuracy of 93% and a Hit@10 transition zone localization of 95%. As the first method to reliably localize the transition zone, this marks a significant step towards the automated identification of this critical clinical landmark.

</details>


### [35] [LayoutLite: Token-Level Implicit Layout Analysis for Efficient Document OCR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22200)
*Xudong Liu,Bicheng Wan,Yulin Jin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LayoutLite is a lightweight plug-and-play module that performs implicit token-level layout analysis to prune redundant visual tokens in VLM-based OCR systems, achieving over 40% reduction in inference cost with negligible quality degradation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: End-to-end OCR systems based on VLMs suffer from efficiency bottlenecks due to the large number of visual tokens generated from document images, many of which correspond to blank margins or visually redundant regions. Generic visual token compression methods risk removing OCR-critical fine-grained details.

Method: LayoutLite aggregates multi-layer visual representations from the vision encoder, predicts an importance score per token using a lightweight scoring network, and removes low-information tokens while preserving spatial positional information. Training is done via reinforcement learning (group-relative policy optimization) driven by OCR output consistency and auxiliary layout supervision, without requiring human annotations.

Result: On OmniDocBench, LayoutLite substantially reduces visual token length and inference cost with negligible recognition quality degradation. On FireRed-OCR and Logics-Parsing-V2, under 50% token compression, it preserves nearly the same score while reducing prefill latency, FLOPs, and KV cache memory by over 40% with minimal additional overhead.

Conclusion: Token-level implicit layout analysis is an effective and practical approach for accelerating VLM-based OCR systems, offering strong efficiency gains without compromising recognition quality.

Abstract: End-to-end OCR systems based on vision-language models have achieved strong performance in complex document OCR, but their efficiency is limited by the large number of visual tokens produced from document images. Many of these tokens correspond to blank margins or visually redundant regions, yet directly applying generic visual token compression methods may remove OCR-critical fine-grained details. In this paper, we propose LayoutLite, a lightweight plug-and-play module for efficient document OCR. Instead of relying on explicit document layout detection, LayoutLite performs implicit layout analysis at the token level between the vision encoder and the language decoder. It aggregates multi-layer visual representations from the vision encoder, and predicts an importance score for each visual token with a lightweight scoring network. Low-information tokens are then removed before entering the language decoder while preserving the original spatial positional information of retained tokens. To train LayoutLite without human annotations, we cast token selection as a reinforcement learning problem and optimize it with a group-relative policy optimization objective driven by OCR output consistency, together with an auxiliary layout supervision signal to stabilize training. Experiments on OmniDocBench demonstrate that LayoutLite can substantially reduce visual token length and inference cost with negligible degradation in recognition quality. We further evaluate LayoutLite on two OCR-specialized VLMs, FireRed-OCR and Logics-Parsing-V2. Under up to 50% token compression, LayoutLite preserves almost the same score on both models while reducing prefill latency, FLOPs, and KV cache memory by over 40%, with only a small additional inference overhead. These results show that token-level implicit layout analysis is an effective and practical approach for accelerating VLM-based OCR systems.

</details>


### [36] [Filling Before Advancing: Capability-Gap-Driven Post-Training for Scenario-Specialized Remote Sensing MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22205)
*Yuheng Zong,Minghua Wang,Xin Zhao,Zhi-Hui Zhan,Antonio Plaza,Jon Atli Benediktsson*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FBA (Filling Before Advancing) is a capability-gap-driven post-training framework for adapting remote sensing multimodal large language models to fine-grained scenario specialization, demonstrated on coastal harbor understanding through a three-stage training process with CPRS dataset and HarborEval benchmark, achieving significant improvements over direct supervised fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Remote sensing MLLMs lack fine-grained scenario specialization due to scarce high-quality data and incomplete capability coverage; existing single-stage SFT fails to address prerequisite capability gaps before target-domain adaptation.

Method: FBA proposes a three-stage training pipeline: (1) RS semantic anchoring for overhead-view visual-language alignment; (2) domain-bridge convergence for shared RS priors across target and bridging scenarios; (3) evidence-grounded scenario tuning for downstream performance, supported by the CPRS three-layer supervision dataset.

Result: On HarborEval benchmark, FBA improves LLaVA-v1.5 from 57.95 (Direct-SFT) to 70.29, and Qwen3-VL from 81.09 to 83.37. FBA also outperforms Collapsed-SFT and leads on harbor-related VRSBench/RSVQA subsets and OpenEval.

Conclusion: Progressive capability gap filling through structured multi-stage post-training is more effective than single-stage SFT for adapting RS-MLLMs to specialized scenarios, with stage-wise and role-replacement analyses validating the approach's design.

Abstract: Remote sensing multimodal large language models (RS-MLLMs) have improved general aerial-image understanding. However, Earth observation applications require fine-grained scenario specialization, constrained by scarce high-quality scenario data and incomplete capability coverage. We formulate this adaptation as a capability-gap-driven post-training problem and propose filling before advancing (FBA). Rather than relying on single-stage supervised fine-tuning (SFT) over target-domain samples, FBA first fills prerequisite capability gaps before advancing toward scenario specialization. We instantiate FBA for coastal harbor understanding, a representative multi-source scenario, by constructing CPRS (Coastal-Port Remote Sensing), a three-layer supervision dataset coupled with three ordered stages: (1) RS semantic anchoring for overhead-view visual-language alignment; (2) domain-bridge convergence for shared RS priors across target and bridging scenarios under different modalities; and (3) evidence-grounded scenario tuning for downstream performance. We construct HarborEval, an eight-track diagnostic benchmark covering perception, spatial understanding, robustness, and generation. Under comparable training budgets, HarborEval increases from 57.95 with Direct-SFT to 70.29 with FBA on LLaVA-v1.5, and from 81.09 to 83.37 on Qwen3-VL. FBA also outperforms Collapsed-SFT and leads on harbor-related VRSBench/RSVQA subsets and OpenEval. Stage-wise and role-replacement analyses validate progressive gap filling and stage-specific roles. Public examples and release updates for CPRS, HarborEval, code, and trained weights are available at https://github.com/Z0ngL1ng/filling-before-advancing.

</details>


### [37] [Deep Convolutional Large-Margin $\ell_p$-SVDD for Visual Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22212)
*Alireza Dastmalchi Saei,Shervin Rahimzadeh Arashloo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DLM-SVDD jointly learns convolutional features and an explicit kernel-based decision boundary via large-margin ℓ_p-SVDD with Frank-Wolfe optimization and CNN updates, achieving strong anomaly detection performance under class imbalance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Visual anomaly detection requires adaptive representations and reliable decision boundaries, but existing methods either use fixed features with principled kernels or learn task-specific features without explicit margin-aware kernel boundaries, especially under scarce anomalies and severe class imbalance.

Method: Proposes DLM-SVDD that jointly learns CNN features and an explicit kernel decision boundary using large-margin ℓ_p-SVDD. Training alternates between Frank-Wolfe-based convex dual boundary updates and CNN updates via a smooth margin-violation loss. Kernel approximation strategies are analyzed for scalability.

Result: Consistent performance improvements over baseline and strong overall results compared to state-of-the-art on multiple benchmarks, with the joint learning scheme remaining effective under severe class imbalance.

Conclusion: Jointly learning representations and kernel decision boundaries with explicit margin maximization is effective for anomaly detection, especially under imbalanced distributions, and scalable via appropriate kernel approximation.

Abstract: Visual anomaly detection requires adaptive representations and reliable decision boundaries, particularly when anomalous training samples are scarce and class distributions are highly imbalanced. Classical kernel-based methods yield principled geometric decision regions but typically operate on fixed features, while deep detectors learn task-specific representations but often fail to provide an explicit margin-aware kernel boundary. In this study, we propose DLM-SVDD, a deep large-margin novelty-detection framework that jointly learns convolutional features and an explicit kernel-based decision boundary. By drawing on the large-margin $\ell_p$-Support Vector Data Description ($\ell_p$-SVDD) approach, the proposed method performs explicit margin maximization and nonlinear slack penalization while adapting the representation to the target task. To train the proposed model, we present an optimization scheme that alternates between a Frank--Wolfe--based update of the convex dual boundary and a CNN update step operating on a smooth margin-violation loss induced by the recovered boundary. To improve scalability, we analyze the efficiency--accuracy trade-offs for different kernel approximation strategies, deriving practical propositions for large-scale anomaly detection.
  Extensive experiments on multiple standard benchmarks show consistent performance improvements over the baseline and strong overall performance compared with state-of-the-art methods while illustrating that the proposed joint representation--boundary learning scheme remains effective under severe imbalanced class distributions.

</details>


### [38] [TRaM-VSR: Importance-Aware Token Routing and Merging for One-Step Diffusion Video Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22231)
*Sicheng Gao,Zhuyun Zhou,Yixuan Liu,Tong Shen,Zongwei Wu,Radu Timofte*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TRaM-VSR accelerates video super-resolution using Diffusion Transformer priors by adaptively routing and merging tokens based on motion and semantic importance, achieving faster inference without sacrificing quality or temporal consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large-scale DiT-based VSR achieves high perceptual quality but suffers from quadratic computational cost from dense spatio-temporal tokens; existing efficiency methods cause irreversible detail loss and temporal flickering, especially in one-step diffusion models.

Method: TRaM-VSR employs Token Routing and Merging (TRaM) with: (1) token importance estimation via motion-sensitive temporal cues fused with semantic text similarity to isolate dynamic objects/structural boundaries; (2) an offline planner that calibrates importance to guide routing across grouped network blocks; (3) within each group, critical tokens processed in a high-fidelity local stream and less informative tokens aggregated into a compact global stream, modulated by network depth and aligned with multigranular diffusion model properties.

Result: Significant inference acceleration while preserving state-of-the-art reconstruction quality and robust temporal consistency.

Conclusion: TRaM-VSR demonstrates that adaptive token allocation using both context-aware video priors and network-level priors effectively addresses the efficiency-quality trade-off in DiT-based VSR.

Abstract: Video super-resolution (VSR) using large-scale Diffusion Transformer (DiT) priors achieves exceptional perceptual quality but is often impractical due to the quadratic computational cost of processing dense spatio-temporal token sequences. Existing efficiency-oriented methods risk irreversible detail loss and temporal flickering, a vulnerability especially pronounced in one-step diffusion models. To address this, we propose TRaM-VSR, a Token Routing and Merging framework for adaptive token allocation, leveraging both context-aware video priors and network-level priors. First, token importance is estimated by fusing motion-sensitive temporal cues with semantic text similarity, isolating dynamic objects and structural boundaries. Next, this importance is further calibrated and adjusted by an offline planner to guide routing across optimally grouped network blocks. Technically, within each routed group, structurally critical tokens are processed in a high-fidelity local stream, while less informative tokens are aggregated into a compact global stream, both modulated by network depth and aligned with the multigranular nature of diffusion models. Extensive experiments show that TRaM-VSR accelerates inference significantly while preserving state-of-the-art reconstruction quality and robust temporal consistency. The code is available at https://github.com/Ree1s/TRaM-VSR.

</details>


### [39] [AgentHOI: Multi-Agent Reasoning for Human-Object-Interaction Video Generation via Implicit Representation Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22241)
*Ziyao Huang,Shunkai Li,Juan Cao,Chenyu Li,Youliang Zhang,Zixiang Zhou,Cong Wang,Yuan Zhou,Qinglin Lu,Fan Tang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AgentHOI is a text-driven human-object interaction video generation framework that uses multi-agent reasoning (perception, interaction, motion planning) to bridge textual intent and physical execution, with an implicit text-motion alignment strategy that distills text-to-motion priors into video diffusion models, eliminating the need for explicit motion inputs at inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing HOI video generation methods rely heavily on explicit motion control, which limits scalability and generalization across diverse objects and interactions. The goal is to enable fine-grained control over interaction logic using only text descriptions.

Method: AgentHOI follows a thinking-before-generation framework with multi-agent reasoning over perception, interaction, and motion planning. It introduces an implicit text-motion alignment strategy that distills text-to-motion priors into the video diffusion model, enabling text-driven HOI synthesis without explicit motion inputs at inference.

Result: AgentHOI significantly improves interaction naturalness, object appearance preservation, and adherence to complex textual instructions in challenging object-centric scenarios such as wearing and riding.

Conclusion: The proposed thinking-before-generation framework with implicit text-motion alignment effectively bridges high-level textual intent and physical execution for HOI video generation, offering improved scalability and generalization over explicit motion control methods.

Abstract: Recent advances in video diffusion models have spurred interest in human-object interaction (HOI) video generation, which demands fine-grained control over interaction logic beyond single-subject animation. However, existing HOI methods rely heavily on explicit motion control, limiting scalability and generalization across diverse objects and interactions. In this study, we propose AgentHOI, a text-driven HOI video generation following a thinking-before-generation framework that bridges the gap between high-level textual intent and physical execution through multi-agent reasoning over perception, interaction, and motion planning. Building upon the generated interaction plans, we further strengthen text-driven motion understanding. We introduce an implicit text-motion alignment strategy that distills text-to-motion priors into the video diffusion model, enabling robust HOI synthesis without explicit motion inputs at inference. Experiments show that AgentHOI significantly improves interaction naturalness, object appearance preservation, and adherence to complex textual instructions across challenging object-centric scenarios such as wearing and riding. The code is available at https://github.com/bone-11/agenthoi.

</details>


### [40] [From level set evolution to threshold optimization: A grayscale level set framework for image segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22255)
*Xingkai Li,Jiebao Sun,Fanghui Song,Zhichang Guo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a fast segmentation framework for degraded images that eliminates the length regularization term in level set methods, proving it unnecessary under certain smoothness constraints, and converts PDE evolution into one-dimensional threshold search for computational efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The length regularization term in level set methods causes numerical instability and high computational cost, making segmentation of multiple degradations challenging.

Method: The authors prove the length term affects the property |∇φ|=1 and is non-essential under smoothness constraints. They define smooth images, construct a grayscale level set, and transform PDE evolution into one-dimensional threshold search.

Result: The proposed framework demonstrates significant advantages in computational speed, especially on large-scale images, with validated segmentation performance on heavily noisy and intensity inhomogeneous images.

Conclusion: The length term can be omitted under smoothness constraints, enabling a faster segmentation framework for degraded images through threshold search instead of PDE evolution.

Abstract: The segmentation of multiple degradations has been a challenging problem in the field of image segmentation. Existing level set approaches commonly adopt a length regularization term to constrain the geometric shape of the segmentation contour. However, the introduction of the length term often results in numerical instability and high computational cost. In this paper, we show that the length term is not essential under certain smoothness constraints, and theoretically prove that the presence of the length term affects the property of $|\nabla φ|=1$. Based on the finding, we define a class of smooth images, construct the grayscale level set, and propose a fast segmentation framework for degraded images, such as heavily noisy images and intensity inhomogeneous images. The framework transforms PDE evolution into one-dimensional threshold search, which has significant advantages in computational speed, especially on large-scale images. Experiments validate the segmentation performance of the proposed framework on various degraded images.

</details>


### [41] [RadSight: Towards Perceptually Reliable Multimodal Radiology Image Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22293)
*Jianqin Liu,Weiwei Cao,Wanxing Chang,Ruifeng Yuan,Bowen Shi,Zhilin Zheng,Xianjie Zhang,Ling Zhang,Peng Wang,Jianpeng Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Medical MLLMs struggle with unreliability due to poor low-level visual perception. The authors introduce Perception-Bench (1.13M samples, 6 dimensions, 2D/3D) to diagnose this, find models fail at basic lesion attributes, then propose RadSight—a dual 2D/3D encoder MLLM with progressive 4-stage curriculum learning on 8.37M corpus—which outperforms existing models across all benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical MLLMs make frequent diagnostic errors due to unreliable visual interpretation. The root cause—poor low-level visual perception (e.g., lesion location, size, density)—is overlooked. The authors aim to systematically trace and address this fundamental bottleneck.

Method: 1) Build Perception-Bench (1.13M samples) evaluating 6 dimensions (attribute judgment, spatial grounding, spatial understanding, disease prediction, anomaly detection, report generation) on 2D/3D radiology. 2) Propose RadSight with dual 2D/3D encoder preserving native spatial structures. 3) Formulate a 4-stage progressive process: visual-language alignment → fine-grained visual perception → clinical diagnosis → diagnostic interpretation. 4) Train on 8.37M perception-oriented corpus with progressive curriculum learning.

Result: RadSight outperforms existing MLLMs across all 6 Perception-Bench dimensions, with large gains in spatial grounding and clinical diagnosis. It also achieves consistent improvements on public 2D and 3D medical benchmarks, demonstrating that robust low-level visual perception is critical for reliable clinical understanding.

Conclusion: Existing medical MLLMs' unreliability stems from a critical bottleneck in low-level visual perception. RadSight's perception-driven design and progressive curriculum learning effectively address this, establishing a strong foundation for trustworthy clinical AI.

Abstract: Medical multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are increasingly expected to perform complex image understanding tasks, yet their reliability is often compromised by frequent errors in visual interpretation. To systematically trace these failures, we traverse the hierarchy from high-level clinical tasks down to fundamental visual perception. We therefore introduce Perception-Bench, a large-scale benchmark comprising 1.13 million samples that assesses medical MLLMs across six dimensions: attribute judgment, spatial grounding, spatial understanding, disease prediction, anomaly detection, and report generation, spanning both 2D and 3D radiology images. Our analysis on Perception-Bench reveals that existing MLLMs lack the ability to capture even the most basic lesion attributes, such as location, size, and density. This inability to ground clinical outputs in primary visual evidence reveals that the models' diagnostic unreliability is rooted in a critical but overlooked bottleneck in low-level visual perception. Motivated by this, we propose RadSight, a perception-driven MLLM built upon a dual 2D/3D encoder architecture that preserves native imaging spatial structures. RadSight formulates medical image understanding as a four-stage progressive process: visual-language alignment, fine-grained visual perception, clinical diagnosis, and diagnostic interpretation. The model is trained on an 8.37 million perception-oriented corpus using progressive curriculum learning. On Perception-Bench, RadSight consistently outperforms existing MLLMs across all six evaluation dimensions, with particularly strong gains in spatial grounding and clinical diagnosis. It also achieves consistent improvements on public 2D and 3D medical benchmarks, further demonstrating that robust low-level visual perception is a critical foundation for reliable clinical understanding. Code and model will be publicly available.

</details>


### [42] [fMRI2Face: A Full-HD fMRI-Video Dataset and Geometry-Guided Neural Decoding Framework for Dynamic Human Face Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22302)
*Jingyang Huo,Xiangru Huang,Chentao Shen,Yikai Wang,Yun Wang,Jianxiong Gao,Shihao Jin,Yanwei Fu,Jianfeng Feng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces fMRI-Face, the first fMRI dataset paired with full-HD digital human facial videos (62,856 samples), and proposes fMRI2Face, a geometry-guided neural video decoding framework that reconstructs dynamic facial videos from brain activity using Brain-derived Appearance Context and Morphable 3D Facial Control integrated with Neural-Controlled Video Diffusion, achieving superior reconstruction fidelity, identity preservation, and motion consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Progress in fMRI-based face decoding is limited by scarce controlled high-resolution neural datasets and methods that struggle to recover both identity-specific appearance and time-varying facial dynamics.

Method: fMRI2Face uses two complementary neural controls: Brain-derived Appearance Context (global identity attributes) and Morphable 3D Facial Control (geometry-aware pose, expression, and dynamics), integrated through Neural-Controlled Video Diffusion with auxiliary latent completion for high-fidelity video reconstruction from fMRI.

Result: fMRI2Face consistently improves reconstruction fidelity, identity preservation, facial geometry, and motion consistency over representative neural decoding baselines, validated on the new fMRI-Face dataset.

Conclusion: fMRI-Face and fMRI2Face establish a controlled platform for studying dynamic face perception and provide a new benchmark for fMRI-based digital human reconstruction.

Abstract: Reconstructing dynamic human faces from brain activity provides a powerful way to study how the mind perceives identity, expression, and facial motion. However, progress in fMRI-based face decoding has been limited by scarce controlled, high-resolution neural datasets and by methods that struggle to recover both identity-specific appearance and time-varying facial dynamics. We present fMRI-Face, the first fMRI dataset paired with controllable full-HD digital human facial videos rendered at 1920$\times$1080 resolution. During scanning, participants watched photorealistic, background-free facial videos with controlled identity, expression, and head pose, while fMRI activity was recorded. The resulting dataset contains 62,856 paired fMRI-video samples, providing a structured resource for studying dynamic face perception and reconstruction. Building on this dataset, we propose fMRI2Face, a geometry-guided neural video decoding framework for reconstructing facial videos from fMRI signals. fMRI2Face derives two complementary neural controls from brain activity: Brain-derived Appearance Context, which captures global identity-related visual attributes, and Morphable 3D Facial Control, which provides explicit geometry-aware guidance for pose, expression, and non-rigid facial dynamics. These controls are integrated through Neural-Controlled Video Diffusion with auxiliary latent completion, enabling high-fidelity facial video reconstruction directly from brain activity. Experiments show that fMRI2Face consistently improves reconstruction fidelity, identity preservation, facial geometry, and motion consistency over representative neural decoding baselines. Together, fMRI-Face and fMRI2Face establish a controlled platform for studying dynamic face perception and provide a new benchmark for fMRI-based digital human reconstruction.

</details>


### [43] [Geometric 2D Scene Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22325)
*Christoph Jahn,Urs Waldmann,Bastian Goldluecke*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A method for constructing scene graphs to represent assembly relationships between components using Faster R-CNN, transformer architecture, and Siamese network with attentional graph convolutional network (aGCN), validated on toy vehicle assembly dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Assembly instructions are essential for production processes and robotics, but existing methods often rely on semantic data and large datasets. The authors aim to create a method that can characterize assembly relationships without semantic data and with very small datasets.

Method: The method uses Faster R-CNN to create geometric representations, processes them through a transformer to generate an adjacency matrix, then feeds this matrix into a Siamese network with message passing based on attentional graph convolutional network (aGCN) to characterize component connections.

Result: Validated on a dataset of toy model components that assemble into transportation vehicles.

Conclusion: The approach successfully constructs scene graphs representing assembly relationships without relying on semantic data, and works effectively with small datasets.

Abstract: In production processes for consumer products, assembly instructions are essential not only for planning but also for executing the production process. Likewise in robotics, it is crucial for an assembly robot to understand how components fit together and can be assembled. To facilitate these tasks, we contribute a method for constructing scene graphs to represent and characterize assembly relationships between components. Our approach does not rely on semantic data and is capable of handling a very small dataset. To realize this, the output of a Faster R-CNN model is used to create geometric representations, which are then processed by a transformer architecture to generate an adjacency matrix. This matrix serves as input to a Siamese network that uses message passing based on an attentional graph convolutional network (aGCN) architecture to characterize the connections between the components. We validate our method on a study dataset of toy model components which can be assembled into transportation vehicles.

</details>


### [44] [SLIP: Segmentation with Low-latency Interactive Prompting for 3D Medical Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22332)
*Baptiste Podvin,Alexandre Ancel,Flavio Milana,Chiara Innocenzi,Davide Arrigo,Federico Espinola Schulze,Guido Torzilli,Jacques Marescaux,Daniel George,Alexandre Hostettler,Toby Collins*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SLIP is an end-to-end trainable framework for interactive 3D medical image segmentation that decouples image encoding from prompt-guided refinement using a lightweight patch memory bank, achieving SOTA performance across 13 public datasets with lower latency, reversible prompting, and superior user preference validated through controlled prospective user studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing patch-based interactive segmentation methods (e.g., nnInteractive) suffer from high interaction latency, limited responsiveness to successive interactions, lack of reversible prompting support, and evaluation relying predominantly on simulated rather than real-user studies.

Method: SLIP decouples image encoding from prompt-guided refinement by computing image features once and reusing them, employing a lightweight patch memory bank that maintains an interaction-aware segmentation state shared across patches, enabling propagation of interaction context throughout the image and supporting reversible prompting without recomputing image features.

Result: Achieves SOTA interactive segmentation performance across 13 public datasets, with lower interaction latency, greater responsiveness, support for reversible prompting, and higher user preference than existing approaches, validated through both standard simulated evaluation and a controlled prospective user study with six expert participants across three clinical annotation tasks.

Conclusion: SLIP addresses key limitations of prior interactive segmentation methods by enabling efficient, responsive, and user-preferred annotation workflows through decoupled architecture and patch memory bank design, while providing rigorous human validation that is often lacking in the field.

Abstract: Interactive deep image segmentation enables efficient medical image annotation by iteratively refining predictions from user prompts, such as positive and negative clicks. Recent patch-based methods, including nnInteractive, achieve strong segmentation performance but remain limited in annotation workflows by high interaction latency, limited responsiveness to successive interactions, and the lack of support for reversible prompting. Furthermore, evaluation relies predominantly on simulated rather than controlled real-user interaction studies. We present SLIP, an end-to-end trainable framework for interactive 3D medical image segmentation that decouples image encoding from prompt-guided refinement. Image features are computed once and reused, while a lightweight patch memory bank maintains an interaction-aware segmentation state shared across patches. This representation enables prediction updates by propagating interaction context throughout the image, supports reversible prompting without recomputing image features, and substantially reduces interaction latency. By separating image representation from interactive reasoning, SLIP remains compatible with a wide range of image encoders. We train a single SLIP model for general interactive segmentation across diverse anatomical structures and imaging modalities. Beyond standard simulated evaluation, we conduct a controlled prospective user study comparing manual segmentation, nnInteractive, and SLIP across three clinical annotation tasks, six expert participants, and subjective usability measures, addressing the limited human validation of interactive segmentation methods. SLIP achieves SOTA interactive segmentation performance across 13 public datasets while providing lower interaction latency, greater responsiveness, support for reversible prompting, and higher user preference than existing approaches.

</details>


### [45] [Time-Reversed Imaging: A Multimodal Benchmark and Framework for Reconstructing Past Human-Environment Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22352)
*Jorge Bacca,Kebin Contreras,Luis Toscano-Palomino,Mauro Dalla Mura*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Time-reversed imaging infers past human-environment interactions from fading multimodal traces (thermal, UV, visible) using the TRACE-HEI dataset and a vision-language-guided diffusion model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video understanding methods focus on extrapolating or interpolating frames forward in time, but cannot infer past events from residual physical imprints that fade over time. The authors want to bridge this gap by inferring past interactions from multimodal traces left behind.

Method: The authors create TRACE-HEI, a tri-modal (thermal, UV, visible) dataset of actions recorded up to 3 minutes post-contact. They propose a multimodal inference approach that extracts structured text descriptions of traces, then uses a vision-language-guided diffusion model to reconstruct plausible past frames.

Result: Inferring recent events from fading traces is challenging but feasible; complementary multimodal modalities reduce solution ambiguity, enabling plausible reconstruction of past interactions.

Conclusion: This work establishes the first computational and experimental foundation for time-reversed imaging, bridging vision, physics, and generative reasoning, opening new directions for scene understanding beyond instantaneous observation.

Abstract: We introduce time-reversed imaging, a new paradigm that infers what just happened in a scene from fading multimodal traces. Instead of extrapolating or interpolating video frames, our goal is to infer past human-environment interactions from residual physical imprints observable in thermal, ultraviolet, and visible spectra. To study this problem, we present TRACE-HEI, the first proof-of-concept dataset for time-reversed imaging, containing synchronized tri-modal video sequences of actions such as sitting, touching, moving objects, and liquid spills, captured across diverse materials and recorded up to three minutes after contact. To establish the benchmark, we propose a multimodal inference approach that extracts structured textual descriptions of detected traces and uses them to constrain a vision-language-guided diffusion model for reconstructing plausible past frames. Experiments show that inferring recent events from fading traces is challenging but feasible when complementary modalities reduce solution ambiguity. This work defines the first computational and experimental foundation for time-reversed imaging, bridging vision, physics, and generative reasoning, and opening new directions for scene understanding beyond instantaneous observation.

</details>


### [46] [SiPhy: Single-Image Physical Property Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22355)
*Hoang Le,Joonwoo Kwon,Elkhan Ismayilzada,Yufei Zhang,Zijun Cui*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SiPhy is a unified framework that infers physical properties (mass, stiffness, elasticity) from a single RGB image by combining 3D-aware visual cues, depth, and language-based material knowledge, achieving state-of-the-art results across multiple benchmarks and outperforming multi-view reconstruction methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches for physical property reasoning rely on multi-view reconstruction or physics-based supervision, which are data-intensive and impractical for single-view scenarios. The goal is to enable physical property inference from a single image to support simulation and embodied AI applications.

Method: From a single RGB image, SiPhy (1) samples pseudo-voxel points, (2) extracts CLIP features and grounds them to material candidates proposed by a VLM, (3) uses a part-based contrastive aggregator for region consistency, and (4) applies a heaviness-aware refinement for better thickness/volume estimation on dense objects.

Result: On ABO-500, MVImgNet-100, and PhysXNet-100, SiPhy achieves SOTA single-image performance: mass MnRE improved by 93% vs. PUGS, density MAE reduced by 35.5% vs. NeRF2Physics, and Young's modulus error lowered by 23.5%. Also validated on real hand-object interaction datasets.

Conclusion: SiPhy demonstrates that single-image physical property reasoning is feasible and effective, surpassing multi-view methods, and shows potential as a data annotation engine for physical understanding from single-view imagery.

Abstract: Inferring physical properties such as mass, stiffness, and elasticity from a single image is essential for simulation and embodied AI, yet most existing approaches rely on multi-view reconstruction or physics-based supervision. We introduce SiPhy, a unified framework for single-image physical property reasoning that aligns 3D-aware visual cues, depth with language-based material knowledge. From one RGB image, SiPhy samples pseudo-voxel points, extracts CLIP features, and grounds them to material candidates proposed by a VLM. A part-based contrastive aggregator enforces region consistency, while a heaviness-aware refinement improves thickness and volume estimation for dense objects. Across ABO-500, MVImgNet-100, and PhysXNet-100, SiPhy achieves state-of-the-art single-image performance, surpassing multi-view reconstruction methods by improving mass MnRE by up to 93% (vs. PUGS), reducing density MAE by 35.5% (vs. NeRF2Physics), and lowering Young's modulus error by 23.5%. We further validate SiPhy on real hand-object interaction datasets, demonstrating its potential as a data annotation engine for physical understanding from single-view imagery.

</details>


### [47] [Active few-shot segmentation by reinforcing data selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22371)
*Chenlan Zhao,Benny Wong,Timothy F. Lundberg,Ahmed M. Elsayed,Abdallah Aljarkas,Hamad A. Aljamaan,Lynn Karam,Qianye Yang,Yipeng Hu,Claire C. Villette,Shaheer U. Saeed*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A reinforcement learning framework for jointly optimizing support-set selection in few-shot medical image segmentation, outperforming random and state-of-the-art methods on pelvic MRI data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing active data selection approaches prioritize samples individually and do not explicitly account for interactions between examples, yet adaptation performance depends strongly on support-set complementarity.

Method: A reinforcement learning framework where an agent directly predicts a support set (jointly optimized rather than via independent scoring) from a pool of unlabelled candidate images to maximize downstream segmentation performance.

Result: Experiments on a cross-institutional pelvic MRI dataset demonstrate improvements over random selection and current state-of-the-art methods.

Conclusion: Support-set complementarity is crucial for effective adaptation, and reinforcement learning shows strong potential for optimizing adaptation sets in few-shot medical image segmentation.

Abstract: Few-shot learning enables medical image segmentation models to adapt to new tasks using only a small number of labelled examples. However, adaptation performance depends strongly on which examples are selected for the support set. Effective support sets should capture relevant variation within the target domain and be informative for adaptation, with constituent samples providing complementary information. Despite this, existing active data selection approaches largely prioritise samples individually and do not explicitly account for interactions between examples. In this work, we propose a reinforcement learning framework for support-set selection in few-shot medical image segmentation, enabling support sets to be optimised jointly rather than through independent sample scoring. Given a pool of unlabelled candidate images, an agent directly predicts a support set that maximises downstream segmentation performance. Experiments on a cross-institutional pelvic MRI dataset demonstrate improvements over random selection and current state-of-the-art methods. Our findings highlight the importance of support-set complementarity for effective adaptation and demonstrate the potential of reinforcement learning for optimising adaptation sets.

</details>


### [48] [IR275K: A Benchmark for Infrared Multi-Frame Super-Resolution Toward Efficient Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22380)
*Jie Deng,Heyang Wang,Changxin Wang,Junkai Shen,Hongyi Chen,Zhiping He,Hongxing Qi,Xudong Zhang,Jianyu Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: IR275K is a new benchmark dataset with 594 infrared video sequences (275,196 frames) for multi-frame super-resolution evaluation, alongside CGMamba, a lightweight SSM model achieving 33.19dB PSNR with efficient X4 super-resolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Infrared remote sensing faces challenges in efficient processing due to constrained detector resolution, power, and downlink bandwidth. MFSR evaluation in infrared sensing is fragmented across private datasets and ad-hoc protocols, with no existing benchmarks capturing thermal contrast, sensor noise, weak texture, and platform-induced frame variation.

Method: Introduced IR275K benchmark with 594 video sequences and 275,196 frames, sequence-level splits, and reproducible X4 evaluation protocol. Proposed CGMamba, a lightweight state-space model (10.90M params, 112.14G FLOPs) combining 2D rotary position encoding (2D RoPE) with center-guided cross-Mamba (CGCM) fusion for implicit multi-frame reconstruction.

Result: CGMamba achieves 33.19dB PSNR, outperforming infrared single-image SR references by 0.35-0.52dB at lower computational cost. Ablation shows removing 2D RoPE causes 1.53dB drop and severe grid-like artifacts.

Conclusion: IR275K provides a reproducible foundation for accuracy-efficiency evaluation of infrared MFSR methods. The architectural analysis highlights that explicit spatial anchoring (2D RoPE) is critical for stabilizing SSM-based cross-frame gating in infrared conditions.

Abstract: Efficient processing is becoming increasingly important in infrared remote sensing, where satellite constellations produce large volumes of observations under constrained detector resolution, power, and downlink bandwidth. Multi-frame super-resolution (MFSR) offers a software-based route to spatial enhancement, but its evaluation in infrared sensing remains fragmented across private datasets and ad-hoc protocols. Existing benchmarks do not explicitly capture the thermal contrast, sensor noise, weak texture, and platform-induced frame-to-frame variation that characterize infrared video. We introduce IR275K, a curated benchmark containing 594 infrared video sequences and 275,196 frames. It provides sequence-level train/validation/test splits and a reproducible X4 evaluation protocol. As an initial architectural probe, we further evaluate CGMamba, a lightweight state-space model with 10.90M parameters and 112.14G FLOPs. CGMamba combines 2D rotary position encoding (2D~RoPE) with center-guided cross-Mamba (CGCM) fusion for implicit multi-frame reconstruction. It achieves 33.19dB PSNR, outperforming infrared single-image super-resolution references by 0.35--0.52~dB at substantially lower computational cost. Ablation results show that removing 2D~RoPE from CGCM causes a 1.53dB drop and severe grid-like artifacts. This indicates that explicit spatial anchoring is critical for stabilizing SSM-based cross-frame gating under infrared conditions. IR275K provides a reproducible foundation for accuracy--efficiency evaluation of infrared MFSR methods, while the architectural analysis offers a concrete starting point for spatially aware SSM design under resource-constrained infrared sensing. Dataset and evaluation resources are available at: https://github.com/InfraRecon7/IR275K.

</details>


### [49] [Correlation-Aware and Gaussianity-Preserving Robust Latent Angular Watermarking for Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22386)
*Yebin Zheng,Haonan An,Guang Hua,Zhiping Lin,Yuguang Fang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LAW is a latent domain watermarking method for diffusion models that embeds watermark bits as antipodal angles between disjoint latent pairs, preserving Gaussianity and achieving robustness against detection/removal attacks. It includes a magnitude-driven variant (LAW-M) for additional robustness and provides theoretical analysis of correlation degradation with closed-form autocorrelation structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing latent domain watermarking methods suffer from: (1) violation of latent Gaussianity, making them prone to watermark detection or removal attacks; (2) sensitivity to normal/malicious perturbations during latent inversion; (3) violation of i.i.d. latent condition causing correlation degradation and generation fidelity loss, which has only been externally measured (e.g., FID) but not rigorously characterized internally.

Method: LAW encodes watermark bits as antipodal angles (±π/2 relative to a reference pair) between disjoint pairs of latent elements, leveraging the rotation-invariant property of isotropic Gaussian to preserve Gaussianity. The angular-error variance is proven proportional to 1/ρ² (inverse squared norm of latent pair). A magnitude-driven variant (LAW-M) anchors bits in the most geometrically stable dimensions for added robustness.

Result: The antipodal encoding (π-separation) maximizes geometric separation between bit values. The decoding angular-error variance is var(Δφ) ∝ 1/ρ². The autocorrelation structure of watermarked latent is derived in closed form, showing correlations are confined to sparse, structured off-diagonal elements with fixed ±π/4 values.

Conclusion: LAW provides a theoretically grounded watermarking method that preserves latent Gaussianity and i.i.d. conditions, with formal characterization of induced correlation degradation and proven robustness through geometric separation and magnitude-driven anchoring.

Abstract: Latent domain watermarking for diffusion models embeds watermarks directly into the latent prior, enjoying non-intrusiveness to model parameters and seamless integration with the generation process. However, due to the violation of latent Gaussianity or sensitivity to normal and malicious perturbations during latent inversion, existing methods are prone to watermark detection or removal attacks. A further overlooked problem is the violation of the i.i.d. latent condition after watermarking, which leads to latent correlation degradation and generation fidelity loss. Although this has been externally measured by FID, the internal correlation structure has yet to be rigorously characterized. To address the above issues, and motivated by the rotation-invariant property of isotropic Gaussian, we propose \textit{Latent Angular Watermarking (LAW)}, which encodes watermark bits as antipodal angles ($\pmπ/2$ relative to a reference pair) between disjoint pairs of latent elements while preserving the Gaussianity. The antipodal ($π$-separation) encoding maximizes geometric separation between bit values, and we prove that the decoding angular-error variance is proportional to the norm of the latent pair, i.e., $\operatorname{var}(Δφ) \propto 1/ρ^2$. We further propose a magnitude-driven variant, LAW-M, which anchors watermark bits in the most geometrically stable latent dimensions, yielding additional robustness gains. Theoretically, we provide a rigorous characterization of the induced correlation degradation, deriving in closed form the autocorrelation structure of the watermarked latent and proving that correlations are confined to a sparse, structured set of off-diagonal elements with fixed $\pmπ/4$ values.

</details>


### [50] [Deformable Triangle Splatting: Flexible Primitives for Real-Time Radiance Field Rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22446)
*Oriol Jiménez-Ayguadé,Antonio Agudo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Deformable Triangle Splatting introduces learnable control points along triangle edges to create non-convex 2D primitives for radiance field rendering, achieving better visual quality than existing convex-only methods while maintaining efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing radiance field methods using 2D primitives (Gaussian disks, triangles) are limited to convex boundaries, requiring excessive primitives to represent curved and concave structures. This work aims to enable non-convex primitive shapes to improve representation efficiency and visual quality.

Method: Each triangle is augmented with K control points per edge, each controlled by a learnable scalar displacement for inward/outward boundary shifts while keeping three base vertices fixed. A differentiable rasterization pipeline operates in barycentric coordinate space, using a winding number test for pixel-in-primitive determination and a window function with learnable sharpness, corner smoothness, and per-primitive opacity for smooth opacity transitions.

Result: Outperforms recent non-volumetric primitive-based methods in visual quality and versatility across various real-world scenes, while maintaining competitive rendering efficiency.

Conclusion: Deformable triangle primitives effectively capture non-convex scene structures, offering a better accuracy-efficiency trade-off than convex-only 2D primitive approaches for radiance field rendering.

Abstract: Recent radiance field methods represent scenes with 2D primitives that offer surface alignment and efficient rasterization, from Gaussian disks to triangles, yet all rely on convex boundaries: curved and concave structures demand excessive primitives. We introduce Deformable Triangle Splatting, which augments each triangle with $K$ control points per edge, each parameterized by a single learnable scalar displacement that shifts the boundary inward or outward, enabling non-convex shape representation while preserving the three base vertices that define the 3D plane. To render these non-convex primitives differentiably, we design a rasterization pipeline in the triangle's barycentric coordinate space, ensuring view-consistent rendering. A winding number test determines whether each pixel lies inside the deformed primitive, and a window function controlled by two learnable parameters, sharpness and corner smoothness, together with a per-primitive scalar opacity, produces the smooth opacity transition from interior to boundary. Validation is done in a variety of real-world scenes, outperforming recent works based on non-volumetric primitives in terms of visual quality and versatility while still achieving competitive rendering efficiency.

</details>


### [51] [Optimal Transport Image Representation and Deep Covariance Alignment (CORAL) for Control Valve Stiction Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22486)
*Seshu K. Damarla*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A stiction detection method combining optimal transport imaging and Deep CORAL alignment to bridge domain shift between simulated training data and real industrial control loops, achieving 90% accuracy and 100% recall on 20 benchmark loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Control valve stiction causes poor control-loop performance in industrial processes. Data-driven methods trained on simulated data fail to generalize to real industrial loops due to domain shift, motivating the need for domain-invariant stiction detection.

Method: Closed-loop signals (controller output and process variable) are converted into 2D optimal transport (OT) images. A CNN encoder is trained with a combined loss: cross-entropy on labeled simulation data and Deep CORAL (covariance-alignment) loss between simulated and unlabeled real industrial data to learn domain-invariant representations.

Result: On 20 benchmark industrial loops: 18/20 correctly diagnosed, 100% recall across all 13 stiction cases, 90.00% accuracy, 92.86% F1-score. Outperforms hand-crafted feature-based baselines.

Conclusion: The proposed OT imaging + Deep CORAL method effectively mitigates domain shift, providing robust and reliable stiction detection for real-world industrial control loops.

Abstract: Control valve stiction is a common cause of unwanted oscillations and poor control-loop performance in industrial processes. Data-driven methods can automatically detect stiction, but models trained purely on simulated data often struggle to generalize to real industrial control loops due to domain shift. To bridge this gap, this work propose a novel stiction detection methodology that combines optimal transport (OT) imaging technique with deep correlation alignment (Deep CORAL) algorithm. Closed loop signals: controller output and process variable are converted into two-dimensional OT images. These images capture the dynamic behaviour of control loops.
